### Result

### Conclusion

Contextuality is a generic property of quantum states, reinforcing its fundamental role in quantum theory and its potential for quantum information processing.

### TL;DR

This paper introduces Shadow Pauli Flow, a new characterization of robust determinism in measurement-based quantum computing with Pauli measurements, which is necessary and sufficient and computable in polynomial time.

### Motivation

The existing Pauli Flow condition is not necessary for robust determinism in MBQC with Pauli measurements, limiting the understanding of when deterministic computations are possible. The authors aim to provide a complete characterization.

### Method

The authors define Shadow Pauli Flow, an extension of Pauli Flow, and prove that an MBQC is robustly deterministic if and only if its correction strategy is consistent with a Shadow Pauli flow. They also provide a polynomial-time algorithm to compute it.

### Result

They prove that Pauli flow is necessary for robust determinism in a weaker sense, but Shadow Pauli Flow is necessary and sufficient in full generality. They also show that Shadow Pauli Flow can be computed in polynomial time.

### Conclusion

Shadow Pauli Flow provides a complete and efficient characterization of robust determinism in MBQC with Pauli measurements, advancing the theory of measurement-based quantum computing.

*Keywords: Measurement-Based Quantum Computing, Pauli Flow, Determinism, Graph States, Quantum Computation*

The method reconstructs drifting error rates and demonstrates improved decoder performance compared to static estimation.

- **Tags**: `measurement`  `noise`  `decoding`  `dynamical stabilizer codes`  `measurement-biased noise`

### TL;DR

This paper optimizes measurement schedules for dynamical color codes under measurement-biased noise, introducing the teraquop volume metric and showing that belief matching decoding can significantly improve performance.

### Motivation

Dynamical stabilizer codes may enable large-scale quantum computation, but their performance depends on the measurement schedule, especially when measurements are noisy. The authors aim to optimize schedules for hardware with noisy readouts.

### Method

They analyze dynamically condensed color codes under various noise biases, using the teraquop volume (product of qubits and measurement rounds) to compare performance. They test repeating measurements and compare belief matching with minimum-weight perfect matching decoders.

### Result

Repeating measurements improves performance under measurement-biased noise but not for unbiased or X-biased noise. The teraquop volume reveals that differences are mainly due to measurement rounds, not qubit count. Belief matching can turn a worst-performing code into a best-performing one.

### Conclusion

The study provides practical guidance for optimizing dynamical codes for noisy hardware, emphasizing the importance of measurement rounds and correlated error decoding.

*Keywords: dynamical stabilizer codes, measurement-biased noise, teraquop volume, belief matching, quantum error correction*

### TL;DR

This paper characterizes the minimum number of two-qubit Clifford operations (CZ-complexity) needed to prepare graph states, linking it to graph transformations and rank-width.

### Motivation

Understanding the resource cost of preparing graph states is crucial for quantum information processing, as graph states are key resources for measurement-based quantum computing and quantum error correction.

### Method

The authors define CZ-complexity and prove a combinatorial characterization of graph-state transformations using vertex deletions, local complementations, and edge-complementations. They then relate CZ-complexity to rank-width and provide algorithms for interval and circle graphs.

### Result

They show that for any graph with n vertices and rank-width r, the CZ-complexity is O(n*r) and at least Ω(n/r) for connected graphs, with bounds close to optimal. They also give O(n) and O(n log n) algorithms for interval and circle graphs, respectively.

### Conclusion

The work provides a deeper understanding of the complexity of graph-state preparation, offering both lower and upper bounds that are nearly tight, and highlights the role of rank-width in this context.

*Keywords: graph states, Clifford circuits, CZ-complexity, rank-width, local complementation*

This paper formulates nonreversible perturbations of Fokker-Planck dynamics as gauge fields that preserve the stationary state while altering relaxation spectra, and connects them to supersymmetric Hamiltonians and learned finite forces.

### Method

The authors introduce a gauge-field formulation for stationary-density-preserving nonreversible perturbations, use similarity transformations to map the Fokker-Planck operator to a scalar Schrödinger-like operator under detailed balance, and analyze spectral deformations.

### Result

### Conclusion

The work provides a theoretical framework for designing nonreversible dynamics with controlled relaxation properties, with potential applications in stochastic thermodynamics and machine learning.

- **Tags**: `fidelity`  `hamiltonian`  `algorithm`  `optimization`  `experiment`

### TL;DR

This paper provides the first provable guarantees for using graph sparsification and decomposition to reduce circuit complexity and improve noise resilience in QAOA for Max-Cut, with applications to trapped-ion and gate-based quantum computers.

### Motivation

QAOA compilation for Max-Cut is expensive and noise-sensitive; graph sparsification and decomposition have been used heuristically but lack theoretical guarantees. The authors aim to provide provable improvements in circuit complexity and noise resilience.

### Method

The authors develop approximate compilation schemes based on graph sparsification (reducing edges while preserving cut structure) and decomposition (breaking weighted graphs into unweighted ones). They analyze worst-case reductions in the number of Molmer-Sorensen pulses and Pauli-X bit flips for trapped-ion simulators, and also provide a generic argument for exponential improvement in circuit fidelity lower bounds for digital gate-based schemes.

### Result

For trapped-ion simulators, they achieve an asymptotic improvement in the number of pulses and bit flips for any constant approximation factor loss. Simulations with dephasing noise show significant improvements in approximation ratio. They also prove exponentially improved circuit fidelity lower bounds for digital schemes.

### Conclusion

These approximate compilation techniques offer provable benefits for reducing circuit complexity and improving noise resilience in QAOA, making them useful tools for near-term quantum computing experiments.

*Keywords: QAOA, graph sparsification, graph decomposition, trapped-ion quantum computing, noise resilience*

The authors aim to determine whether quantum computers can provide exponential speedup for simulating classical systems with local interactions, which are common in real-world partial differential equations, as opposed to the previously studied long-range interactions.

### Method

## 386. The Paradox Of Steady-state Light Diffusion

- **Score**: 1.5 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ❌ No
- **Source**: Rep. Prog. Phys.  |  **Feed**: Reports on Progress in Physics (ROPP)
- **Direction**: General / Other
- **Published**: 2026-08-10
- **Authors**: André Liemert, Lorenzo Pattelli, Alwin Kienle, Federico Tommasi And Fabrizio Martelli
- **Link**: [https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1361-6633/ae8eaa](https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1361-6633/ae8eaa)
- **Tags**: `transport`  `scattering`  `light diffusion`  `radiative transfer equation`  `diffusion coefficient`

### TL;DR

This paper resolves the long-standing controversy over the definition of the diffusion coefficient in light transport, proving that in the continuous-wave regime no scalar diffusion coefficient can make diffusion asymptotically equivalent to the radiative transfer equation when absorption is nonzero.

### Motivation

The definition of the diffusion coefficient in light transport has been debated for decades, with absorption-dependent formulations violating fundamental scaling properties. The authors aim to resolve this controversy by providing exact consistency tests.

### Method

The authors use analytical proofs based on the requirement that diffusion converges to the radiative transfer equation (RTE) in the limit of infinite propagation. They analyze both time-domain and continuous-wave regimes, deriving conditions for asymptotic equivalence.

### Result

In the time domain, the absorption-independent coefficient is necessary and sufficient for exact long-time convergence. In the continuous-wave regime, no scalar diffusion coefficient can make diffusion asymptotically equivalent to the RTE when absorption is nonzero, as matching the dominant attenuation length and the residue require different coefficients.

### Conclusion

The time-domain formulation is the only self-consistent reference for diffusion in optical transport, and the paper calls for re-examination of how diffusion is applied in optical transport studies.

*Keywords: light diffusion, radiative transfer equation, diffusion coefficient, absorption, continuous-wave regime*

---
